**Custody.** Week on, week off, changing on Sundays. Jo's weeks start May 10, May 24, June 7 and June 21, and every other week after. On her Sunnyvale Saturdays Kevin keeps Owen overnight (ch 5); that fails on June 13, when Kevin has graduation at his school (ch 11). Owen is at Kevin's the weeks of May 31 and June 14.

**Saturdays in Sunnyvale.** The crew starts at seven. Four hours, seven to eleven, is all the time Cal has ("Everything else is booked through September," Todd), and the pace is about a room a Saturday with him deciding, which is why it takes ten. The crew's lunch is at eleven, when he is supposed to leave. On the first Saturday he stays through lunch and decides the coats ("You were done at eleven." "I know what time it is."), so "bring a lunch" means stay. The crew is done by about one, and Jo is home in San Bruno by two (ch 5). A client does not carry, and Jo says so: two paid men, and her insurance. He does not carry on the first Saturday. His carrying starts with Frank's ramp (ch 6) and the hutch (ch 7: she refuses, allows one piece on her terms, and keeps him on the second against her own word; that is what Vili's "helping, or a client?" is about). Any later carrying (ch 15, ch 34) needs its own reason. Ten of the fifteen Saturdays from May 16 to August 22 are in Sunnyvale: May 16, 23 and 30; June 6, 13, 20 and 27; July 25; and August 15 and 22.

## Places

**The house on West Remington Drive** (Todd, who lived on Remington). Darlene's house is an old Eichler on West Remington near Bernardo, west of Mary: a wide, tree-lined street, with several beautiful Eichlers near the church and across from the school. Stockton was ruled out, because a South City mover would not run a crew that far every week.
- The church is the Congregational Church of Sunnyvale, on the [southeast corner of Remington and Bernardo](https://uufs.org/about-us/directions) (1112 South Bernardo).
- The school is [Sunnyvale Middle](https://en-academic.com/dic.nsf/enwiki/2105875), which opened in the 1960s as Mango Junior High, became Sunnyvale Junior High (grades 7 and 8) in 1982 and a 6-8 middle school in the 1990s. Darlene worked its cafeteria line for thirty years and handed Cal his tray there (Claude's addition, on the page since ch 5). It feeds line nine.
- Eichlers at that end date from 1960 to 1962 (Jones & Emmons and Claude Oakland atrium models, about 1,700 square feet); two sold for [$3.5 million](https://atriare.com/properties/1194pimentoave) and [$3.2 million](https://atriare.com/properties/1166maraschinodr) in 2024 and 2023. East of Mary is the [Fairbrae Addition](https://www.eichlerforsale.com/fairbrae-eichlers/), built 1958 to 1961. All are one story on a slab, courtyard and atrium models; Sunnyvale Eichlers sell for [$2.1 to $3.4 million](https://www.boyengarealestateteam.com/blog/top-5-eichler-neighborhoods-in-sunnyvale-ca-a-property-nerds-guide) now.
- No stairs, so the hutch (ch 7) and the piano (ch 34) go up the truck ramp, and lunch is on the bench in the atrium. Eleven doors, nine of them sliding (ch 4).
- Glass walls and heat in the floor, with no ducts, so there is no air conditioning to die in ch 21; the heat wave does the work.
- Her yard in South San Francisco is [30.6 miles and about 33 minutes](https://www.rome2rio.com/s/South-San-Francisco/Sunnyvale-CA-USA) away; his house in Woodside is [15.4 miles and 22 minutes](https://www.rome2rio.com/s/Woodside-CA-USA/Sunnyvale-CA-USA) away. He has no excuse of distance. South City stays. Todd asked on October 9 whether it is realistic for the Saturdays and Los Gatos. Peninsula movers work down to San Jose and bill the drive; the Saturdays are about forty minutes down 101 at 6:15; Los Gatos comes up only in 8, 9 and 16; San Bruno to Woodside is twenty-odd minutes. Vili says the drive out loud in 20.
- [Libby's cannery](https://www.atlasobscura.com/places/libby-s-water-tower) opened in 1906, was the largest in the world by 1922 and the town's main employer, and closed in 1985. Its water tower, painted as a can of fruit cocktail, still stands in an office park.
- The Community Center on East Remington is wrapped in a working orchard of [800 Blenheim apricot trees](https://www.atlasobscura.com/places/orchard-heritage-park). The fruit ripens in early July and is sold from a stand in the parking lot.
- The trees on the block between Mary and Bernardo are not found, so chapter 4 says only "old trees" until Todd names them. A [2022 tour by Sunnyvale Urban Forest Advocates](https://www.sunnyvaletrees.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/TOD-Car-Bicycle-Tour.pdf) covers West Remington from Hollenbeck to Mary (carob trees, then cork oaks just before Mary) and East Remington by the Community Center (tall liquidambars, Italian stone pines). Citywide the commonest street trees are [southern magnolia, sweetgum and Chinese pistache](https://sunnyvaletrees.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/09/Urban-Forest-Mgmt-Plan-2014-Adoptied-9-16-2014-web-minimized.pdf).
- Claude's additions, on the page since ch 4: her father bought the house new in 1960 and she inherited it with a loan against it ($612 a month until Cal paid it off in 2012); the neighbor is Pat Dolan, 79, who kept the boxes, the mail and a spare key.

**Where they go** (Todd's local ground; each place does a job in the story)
- **The Manresa room, Los Gatos (ch 8).** The first dinner, booked by Janice. Manresa closed on December 31, 2022; the room reopened in 2024 as Ritual at Manresa, a members' club: a visiting chef cooks one week a month, Wednesday to Sunday at 6:30, ten to twelve courses; non-member tables are $450 or $530 a person; nothing is listed after July 2026, so the dinner sits in the June week.
- **The club's parking lot and a taco truck, Los Gatos (ch 9).** The truck is invented and is called Tacos Arreola; Todd's idea came from Bravos Tacos, a real truck in Los Gatos, so its name is not used. No truck called Tacos Arreola turned up in a Bay Area map search or a web search. Remy hires it to come to the lot, paid till eleven. The restaurant's listing shows a free parking lot, and the curb she stands on is in it. The chapter gives only the name on its side, a window, a board, a generator and a cook, because what it looks like, serves and charges could not be checked. A dental chair weighs about 300 pounds.
- **Summit Road, Santa Cruz Mountains (ch 16).** Napa is where Remy sent the other eleven. The villa is unnamed; Silver Mountain is named (Todd, October 11). The real ground: Burrell School, in the 1890 schoolhouse, takes walk-ins and closes some Saturdays for weddings; Regale, next door, is members only by reservation; the Summit Store is across the road and closes at 7; downtown Los Gatos streets have a two-hour limit. Chapter 16 also uses Todd's corrections (October 8): Regale, not the schoolhouse, has the lawn for a wedding; nobody drives up for one winery, and people stop at the Summit Store while they taste, never on a second trip up from Los Gatos, a good twenty minutes down; the store's tri tip sandwiches are made ahead and taken from the case, with nothing to order; Wrights Station, down the next turnoff, is pretty, a 1947 farmhouse with vines against the redwoods ([Edible Silicon Valley](https://ediblesiliconvalley.ediblecommunities.com/drink/summit-road-wine-trail)); Burrell School has a sunny deck over the vines and welcomes picnics, but the book no longer uses it. In Los Gatos they park by Vasona Lake and walk the creek trail north to the Los Gatos Creek County Dog Park, which has separate sides for small and large dogs ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Gatos_Creek_Park)). Lexington Reservoir, beside 17, is full (Todd); the Los Gatos Rowing Club rows there, with weekend practices early in the morning ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Gatos_Rowing_Club)), so Jo has seen the crews at six on her Summit Road jobs. The Los Gatos parking ticket is out of the chapter.
- **Silver Mountain Vineyards, above Summit Road (ch 16).** Todd moved the tasting here on October 11. It is at 269 Silver Mountain Drive, about two miles from the store by Soquel San Jose Road, Miller Cut Off and Miller Hill. It sits on a 2,100-foot ridge looking over Monterey Bay and the redwood mountains, with a deck, solar panels on the winery roof, open Saturday and Sunday noon to five, and picnics welcome ([San Jose](https://www.sanjose.org/listings/silver-mountain-vineyards), [7x7](https://7x7.com/amp/santa-cruz-mountains-wine-tasting-2453999646), [Good Times](https://www.goodtimes.sc/?p=104368)). The founder, an Air Force pilot turned commodities trader, bought an abandoned 18-acre orchard there in 1979; floods and landslides in the early 1980s, the 1985 Lexington fire and the 1989 Loma Prieta earthquake, which destroyed the winery, followed; he reopened in 1995; he farms organic, for less fruit and more flavor ([Entrepreneur](https://www.entrepreneur.com/business-news/silver-is-better-than-gold/203294)). The estate vineyard started in 1981 with Chardonnay cuttings from Mount Eden, put straight into the ground with no rootstock; he holds reds five or six years and is adding Bordeaux varieties as the climate warms ([Edible Monterey Bay](https://www.ediblemontereybay.com/blog/43-years-at-silver-mountain)). The Pinot Noir and Chardonnay have won medals ([Good Times](https://www.goodtimes.sc/?p=59670)). Todd (October 11): the owner is blunt but loves talking about his wine, and stays behind the bar; a winery dog always greets visitors and owns the place, so nobody takes its collar; you pay after tasting, and buying wine takes the tasting off. The owner is a real person, so the book never names him and gives him only what is on record and two blunt lines. On the way up from 17, Summit Road passes the Taylor Ranch orchard, whose stand runs on an honor box, and Christ Child church, a small parish church in the trees. In real weather June 27, 2026 was clear over the bay by early afternoon; San Jose reached 71 and SFO 66, so Los Gatos is 73 and San Bruno 64 ([Iowa Mesonet](https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/request/download.phtml)).
- **The Big Island and Maui (ch 19; Todd, October 9).** Kīlauea erupted in episodes through the summer of 2026, a lava fountain every week or two, each lasting hours, watched by the USGS Hawaiian Volcano Observatory. In the record, episode 51 was July 15 and episode 52 began the evening of July 28, so no fountain fell in the trip (July 20 to 22). Todd chose to invent one (October 9: "I want the volcano performing a show for the readers, it does not need to be historically accurate as you don't need to say which one it is"): it goes on Tuesday evening, July 21, and the chapter never numbers it; episode 53 ended August 13 and episode 54 on August 25 ([Hawaii Guide](https://www.hawaii-guide.com/blog/kilauea-episode-54-lava-fountaining-begins)). No airship flies in Hawaii, and the airspace over an eruption is closed to tours, so his people do it in the background: an airship leased and shipped to the Big Island (it cannot fly the ocean), the access (a promise to the governor of a tech center), and a world-famous volcanologist, invented, as their guide. They stay upwind and off the fountain. His house on Maui, never visited, is not in Lahaina; a caretaker keeps it ready. Todd: "Don't get caught in the details. This can all just happen in the background done by his minions." The house is on the south shore past the hotels, unnamed, with Molokini's crescent and Kahoʻolawe offshore and green sea turtles on the reef in front. Mount St. Helens, May 18, 1980: about 2,500 motorists were stranded in Ritzville, more than the town's population, in school gyms, churches and restaurants ([HistoryLink](https://www.historylink.org/file/9398)).
- **The bay, out under the Golden Gate (ch 23).** Frank's sloop out of Oyster Point; Frank's boat and the yearly sail for Jo's mother are Claude's additions. About fourteen nautical miles each way by the researcher's own measure, seven to ten hours dock to dock; the ebb under the bridge can pass six knots; a 1976 Catalina 27 fits. Check the route on a chart, and the tide for Monday, July 27.
- **Castle Rock State Park, on Skyline (ch 26; Todd's suggestion).** On Highway 35, two and a half miles southeast of Highway 9, $10 to park. The falls drop 75 feet and may be a trickle in a dry month. Goat Rock looks over the San Lorenzo Valley to the ocean. Most of the climbing is bouldering on pocketed sandstone. No dogs. No cell signal (Todd confirmed). The Kirkwood entrance lot ($10 at self-serve kiosks) has a gate locked every day at sunset, and the old lot a quarter mile south, nearest Castle Rock, is for overnight guests with a gate code ([Sempervirens Fund](https://sempervirens.org/visit/castle-rock-state-park/)); day use runs 6 a.m. to sunset ([State Parks](https://www.parks.ca.gov/?page_id=538)). In ch 26 Jo parks on the shoulder of Skyline. Goat Rock is a 100-foot sandstone monolith at 2,850 feet; Ben Lomond Mountain's ridge blocks most of the ocean, with parts of Santa Cruz and Monterey Bay in view ([SummitPost](https://www.summitpost.org/goat-rock/193294)). Sunset on July 30, 2026 is about 8:18.
- **Alice's Restaurant, Sky Londa (ch 26; Todd's suggestion).** 17288 Skyline Boulevard, where Highway 84 crosses Skyline, on the drive from Woodside to Castle Rock. Built in 1918 as a general store for loggers, a cafe by the 1940s; bought in 1960 by the woman it is named for, and the song came later (1967), though many believe it is named for the song ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Londa,_California), [M-A Chronicle](https://machronicle.com/?p=183662), [Almanac](https://www.almanacnews.com/news/2024/01/12/blog-from-logger-supplies-to-our-own-alices-restaurant/)). Wooden inside, booths, a bar, a veranda, picnic tables. Motorcyclists, cyclists and car people stop there; the big crowd is weekend mornings. Open 8 a.m. to 8 p.m. Monday to Saturday and 8 a.m. to 7 p.m. on Sunday (Todd), so it is closed when they come back down on Thursday night. Known for its burgers, named for motor brands: the BMW with Wagyu, the Norton (blue cheese and bacon) and the Jalopy (Todd; [Tillamook County Pioneer](https://tillamookcountypioneer.net/take-a-trip-to-alices)). The owner is a real person and is not named. In ch 26 Jo stops there on the way up at about 5:02.
- **Highway 1, Carmel Beach and Nepenthe (ch 27).** Dogs may be off leash on Carmel Beach under voice control; the foot of Ocean Avenue is a sand ramp, not stairs; nothing over 7.5 feet tall or 20 feet long parks on Scenic Road ([City of Carmel](https://ci.carmel.ca.us/post/beach-rules)). The main beach lot is at the bottom of Ocean Avenue, with restrooms near it (City of Carmel); Todd: it has a bathroom and a straight shot down a slope to the beach, and it is where people congregate. Carmel houses have no numbers, and an address reads like "San Antonio 3 SE of 9th" ([AP, 2024](https://www.news4jax.com/news/politics/2024/08/11/where-do-you-live-thats-a-complicated-question-for-a-california-town-with-no-street-addresses/)). Nepenthe is walk-in only, about 808 feet over the ocean, with a terrace, a fire pit and built-in bleachers ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepenthe_(restaurant)), [Islands](https://www.islands.com/1896509/nepenthe-cafe-kevah-big-sur-california-historic-cliffside-restaurant-unmatched-views-gourmet-fare/)); the menu has a Basket of French Fries at $15.75, the Famous Ambrosiaburger at $27 (a ground steak sandwich on a French roll with Ambrosia sauce and a choice of garbanzo-kidney bean salad, coleslaw or green salad), bottled North Coast Red Seal Ale at $13.75, and the Four-Layer Chocolate Fudge Cake at $16.25, dark chocolate cake with semi-sweet ganache, created about 1990 by Holly Fassett when she was a server ([Nepenthe menu](https://www.nepenthe.com/menu), [Nob Hill Gazette](https://www.nobhillgazette.com/food_and_wine/dining/one-favorite-recipe-nepenthe-s-four-layer-chocolate-fudge-cake/article_6f55d324-7b8d-45b6-baa4-ca68d2840357.html)). Dogs are not allowed at Nepenthe or Café Kevah (they are welcome in the Phoenix Shop), so Pancake waits in the truck in the shade. On August 1, 2026 Monterey was overcast at 300 to 700 feet with mist until about 11, 57 degrees at dawn, scattered clouds at midday and clear by mid-afternoon; Half Moon Bay was overcast with mist until about 9 (Iowa Mesonet); low tide at Monterey was 7:04 a.m. (NOAA). Woodside to San Gregorio over 84 is about 45 minutes, San Gregorio to Carmel about 1 hour 50, Carmel to Nepenthe about 50 minutes (OSRM). The Timber Fire closed Nepenthe and Highway 1 from August 10, 2026, so the scene sits on the first weekend of August.
- **Carmel Beach, Scenic Road and the way home (ch 27).** Carmel Beach has surfers and pelicans, with Pebble Beach Golf Links at its north end ([Carmel California](https://www.carmelcalifornia.com/famous-carmel-beach/)); seals and sea glass there are Todd's. Bixby Bridge opened in 1932; it is 714 feet long with a 360-foot main span and 260 feet of clearance, built on a timber falsework of over 300,000 board feet of Douglas fir; it is one of the most photographed bridges in California and has appeared in numerous auto commercials; the pullout is on the north and west side, and a small beach lies beneath it at the creek mouth ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bixby_Bridge)). A landscape painter called Point Lobos the "greatest meeting of land and water in the world" ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_Lobos)); he is a real person and is not named. Pebble Beach's 10th green sits on a grassy, rocky, sandy bluff above Carmel Beach, the southernmost point of Pebble Beach, about a quarter mile north along the beach, with signs at its base against climbing it ([Golf.com](https://golf.com/news/tournaments/pebble-beach-10th-hole-secret-enterance/)); the 10th slopes toward Carmel Bay on the right ([Golf Monthly](https://www.golfmonthly.com/microsites/hole-by-hole/pebble-beach-hole-by-hole-guide-hole-10-15116)). The chapter names it: "the Pebble Beach golf course" (Todd, October 14: "It's famous."). Cobalt blue sea glass runs about one piece in 200 to 300, a collector-jeweler's estimate ([By The Sea Jewelry](https://bytheseajewelry.com/the-colors-of-sea-glass/)); Richard LaMotte ranks cobalt and cornflower blue as rare ([MV Times](https://www.mvtimes.com/2014/12/09/gifts-nature-sea-glass/)). Scenic Road runs one way south from the foot of Ocean Avenue, about a mile and a half along the bay to Mara Beach ([Gribblenation](https://www.gribblenation.org/2025/01/scenic-road-carmel-by-sea.html)). On Carmel Point a poet's stone house and its 40-foot tower stand; he set the tower's stones himself from 1920 to late 1924, some boulders about 400 pounds, from the beach below ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tor_House)); the poet is a real person and is not named. The Mission is on Rio Road. Highway 1 was closed near Regent's Slide, south of Esalen, as of February 2026 ([Caltrans](https://dot.ca.gov/caltrans-near-me/district-5/d5-news/d5-news-2-17-2026)), so the way home from Nepenthe is back up 1.
- **Point Lobos and Alila Ventana (ch 27; Todd's).** Dogs are not allowed in Point Lobos, "including in vehicles"; it is $10 a car, opens at 8, and asks visitors to park only in designated pullouts on Highway 1, not on the shoulder ([Point Lobos Foundation](https://www.pointlobos.org/info/)). The Cypress Grove Trail is a 0.8-mile loop of about half an hour, and visitors stay within the guide wires; the rust-orange on the trees and rocks is an alga, Trentepohlia, colored by carotene, "the same pigment that gives color to carrots"; the only other native grove of Monterey cypress is at Cypress Point; California sea lions haul out on the offshore rocks ([trail guide](https://www.pointlobos.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/Cypress-Grove-Trail-2018.pdf)). Alila Ventana Big Sur, 48123 Highway One, just north of Nepenthe, is tailored to adults, checks in at four, includes dinner on arrival and breakfast on departure, and has complimentary valet ([Hyatt](https://www.hyatt.com/alila-hotels-and-resorts/en-US/sjcal-ventana-big-sur/policies)). Dogs are $150 a stay, two at most, with a dog bed and water bowl in the room, and may eat on the Sur House terrace but not indoors ([Ventana](https://www.ventanabigsur.com/resort/dog-friendly)); the dog's menu has Chicken & Rice $12, Coco Patty $12 and Salmon Bowl $14 ([dog brochure](https://www.ventanabigsur.com/pdf/dog-friendly-ebrochure.pdf)). The rooms are wood-paneled with wood-burning fireplaces and private decks ([Ventana](https://www.ventanabigsur.com/stay/suites)); the fires come as bags that burn 30 to 45 minutes, and four inclusive nights in a standard room ran about $14,760 ([Travel on Points](https://travel-on-points.com/review-alila-ventana-big-sur/)), so a night is most of Hillsborough's $4,860.
- **Sweeney Ridge (ch 28).** Golden Gate National Recreation Area, 1,200 feet, between San Bruno and Pacifica; views from the Farallones and Tamalpais to Diablo; hawks and deer; winds common; dogs on leash ([NPS](https://www.nps.gov/places/000/sweeney-ridge.htm)). From the park's corner of Skyline College's Lot 2, a fire road goes around a coastal-scrub hill to old Nike foundations at three quarters of a mile, drops steeply into a ravine and climbs out, meets the Mori Ridge Trail at a mile, reaches the old radar station at a mile and a half, and runs half a mile of paved fire road to the Discovery Site; about four miles round trip and 600 feet of gain; fog and strong afternoon wind are common ([TrailMaster](https://www.thetrailmaster.com/hike/sweeney-ridge/)). The radar buildings have cement-block walls and falling-in roofs; the paved road passes a water tank, and a gravel path runs a few hundred feet to the site, where a granite post helps identify the mountains around the bay ([Greenbelt Alliance](https://www.greenbelt.org/blog/urban-outing-sweeney-ridge-discover-views-of-bay-like-1769)). Nike site SF-51 had its radar on Sweeney Ridge and its launchers on Milagra Ridge; by the 1960s nuclear-capable Nike Hercules could launch on a minute's notice; obsolete by 1974 (NPS). The Portolá party, hungry, lost and sick, was guided up the ridge by the Aramai of present-day Pacifica and became the first Europeans to see the bay, on November 4, 1769 (NPS); they had left San Diego on July 14 looking for Monterey and passed it without knowing it ([smccd.edu](https://accounts.smccd.edu/case/sweeneyridge/intro.html)). A paved road comes up from Sneath Lane, closed to cars. August 5, 2026: Half Moon Bay overcast near 1,100 feet from 5:15 p.m., SFO clear, west-northwest wind 11 to 17 knots, sunset 8:13, civil dusk about 8:42 ([Iowa Environmental Mesonet](https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/)). August 6 at 4 p.m., SFO west-northwest 15 knots gusting 23 (same source). Heavy departures sometimes climb west off 28L and 28R; arrivals line up over the bay. Poison oak is common off the trails (Greenbelt Alliance).
- **Westlake, Daly City (ch 29).** Built after the Second World War by one developer, a real person who is not named; the living space is upstairs and the ground floor is a garage; pre-cut, pre-labeled parts; lots of about 3,300 square feet; ceilings rarely over eight feet ([KQED](https://kqed.org/news/11882379/how-daly-city-became-one-of-the-most-densely-populated-cities-in-the-country)). The colors, the inside stairs and Sharon's father buying new in 1951 are the chapter's. Jo's first Westlake house since Maui was in July.
- **Cal's floor (ch 29).** Sixty desks with nothing between them; Janice's office at the far end with his door inside it; her fourteen cabinets go twenty-two feet into his office, where the long wall is nineteen feet and fourteen cabinets at fifteen inches take seventeen and a half. Level two of his garage, nose out. All Claude's.
- **Her driveway, before dawn, August 12 (ch 29).** SFO clear at 4:40 a.m., 14°C (57°F), light northwest wind; scattered low cloud at 1,300 feet from about 5:55 and broken at 1,400 by 6:38 ([Iowa Mesonet](https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/)). New moon; the Perseids' broad peak runs from the evening of August 12 into the 13th ([Star Walk](https://starwalk.space/en/news/all-you-need-to-know-about-the-perseids)).
- **The zeppelin (ch 7, 16 and the bonus scene).** No passenger airship flies here now: Airship Ventures flew from Moffett Field from 2008 to 2012, and Pathfinder 1 is test-flying with no passengers. Rides began in October 2008 at about $500 an hour, and routes went up 101 toward San Francisco ([CNN Money, 2009](https://money.cnn.com/2009/04/09/smallbusiness/helium_heads.fsb/index.htm)); Hangar One stands east of 101 in Mountain View ([KQED](https://www.kqed.org/news/11738379)). So it is a memory in chapter 16. In chapter 19 it comes true: Cal has an airship over Kīlauea for Owen's volcano, and Jo rides the zeppelin she watched in 2009. The newsletter bonus scene (an airship over Moffett again, and Cal asks if she wants a ride) can stay or go.
- The meals get plainer as they get closer: twelve courses in the Manresa room (8), tri tips from the Summit Store, which she pays for (16), his terrible breakfast (17), burgers on the terrace at Nepenthe, which she pays for, and one piece of cake split down the middle, which he asks to pay for (27; the fries became a real lunch on Todd's note, October 15).

## Research decisions

The full report is claude/research-notes.md; what only a visit can settle is listed at its end.
- Jo refinanced in 2023 at seven percent to buy Kevin out; her promissory note to Cal carries the same rate. The numbers on the page in ch 18 come from a $600,000 thirty-year loan at 7.000 percent, first payment December 2023 (principal and interest $3,991.81), with $1,900 extra a month from January 2024. That leaves $518,395.69 after the July 2026 payment and pays the loan off in November 2036, the month she turns 55. Chapters 27 to 29 use the same loan.
- Darlene's house is in a living trust with Cal as trustee.
- "Low man calls the lift" is Frank's rule, not the trade's.
- Anyone can pay off another person's mortgage. It is a gift, and the payer cannot deduct it; Janice says so when she refuses in ch 28. Only the borrower can get the exact payoff figure, so he sends the balance and a cushion, and the lender refunds the rest.
- Dawn does not use NDAs; the fourteen pages in ch 25 are the first Jo has seen.
- A premarital agreement in California has to be in writing and signed by both of them (Family Code section 1611), so Stephanie's draft binds no one. It is her fallback. One can be thrown out if it was unconscionable and the other side's property and debts were not fairly disclosed, so a lawyer's draft carries a schedule for each side. Her lawyer filled in Cal's and left Jo's for Jo. Each of them also needs seven days with the final version before signing, and either a lawyer or a written waiver of one (section 1615). Ch 31.
- Stephanie was born about 1985, when it was the sixth most common name for girls born in the United States, by Social Security data (ch 22).
- Kīlauea in July 2026 (ch 19): the record has episode 51 on July 15 and episode 52 on July 28, with no fountain July 20 to 22. Todd chose to invent a fountain on Tuesday evening, July 21 (October 9), shown and never numbered. What the show uses is real in kind: the summit swells back up before each episode; an episode opens with gas pistoning, spatter and overflows from the north vent, then sustained fountaining; fountains in 2025 and 2026 reached about 1,000 to 1,500 feet, sometimes from both vents at once, with a roar, lava rivers on the crater floor, a plume lit from below, Pele's hair on the wind, and an abrupt end. The eruption's episodes began December 23, 2024, and by July 19 there had been fifty-one.
- The airship (ch 19) is the size of a Zeppelin NT: 75 meters (246 feet), nine of Jo's 26-foot trucks with some left over; semi-rigid, with a frame inside; two pilots. Its make is never named.
- California's basic mover liability is sixty cents a pound; damage over $1,000 goes to the DMV on form SR-1 within ten days (ch 10).
- The pufferfish: a fish as long as your hand works a week on a sand circle wider than a grown-up is tall, for one mate, and starts a new one every time.
- Jo's purse holds a tape measure, green tape and a carpenter's pencil. The valet at the Palace Hotel wants $110 for her truck, so she parks in a yellow zone on Minna Street.
- Mortgage burning (ch 29): a common mid-century celebration with friends and neighbors; the standard advice is to keep the original note and burn a copy ([Banker & Tradesman](https://bankerandtradesman.com/dont-burn-your-mortgage/)).
- The payoff (ch 29): after her August payment the principal is about $515,527.85; ten days at $98.87 make a payoff of $516,516.55 on August 10, so the $521,000 wire leaves $4,483.45 to refund. The loan has 156 payments, December 2023 to November 2036. A wire confirmed Friday after the cutoff lands Monday. Only the borrower can ask; the servicer cannot reverse a payoff, and the sender would have to start a recall with his own bank.

## Voice rules

The style guide (claude/romance-style-guide.md) has the full sentence rules, including Todd's October 8 rule on narration. These are the rules for keeping people on the page.
- **The three-line test.** Cover the chapter heading; a reader should know whose chapter it is within three lines.
- **Each lead has a vocabulary.** Jo thinks in loads, stairs, hours and payroll. Cal thinks in specs, tests, edge cases and confidence. Neither borrows the other's words until late; the first time Cal says "call it" unprompted is a beat. Strap imagery is limited to Vili's one speech in ch 7 and the lines already on the page (Todd).
- **Each has a sentence they cannot say.** Jo never says "I need." Cal never says sorry without a fix attached. Each says it once, near the end.
- **Cal speaks in whole sentences, with no contractions** (Todd). His narration has no contractions either. The short lines he may say are yes, no, "Noted," "Understood," "All right," "Thank you," a greeting or a name, a pick between options he was just offered, and a line of his list read aloud. The texts he types himself have no contractions either. Every other character uses contractions.
- **Nobody answers the question asked.** People deflect, mishear and change the subject. Kevin answers every question with a schedule.
- **Every scene has a third person who wants something.** Vili wants his Saturday. Owen wants the dog. Janice wants the thermostat on an even number.
- **One trade detail per scene,** used and never explained.
- **Nobody states the theme,** the agent least of all: it can talk, sing and flatter, and it never understands.
- **The texts have two authors.** Janice names the flowers and remembers the sister, with one semicolon and a practical last line. Cal writes four words with a number in them. The reader can tell them apart before Jo can. No new Janice messages after chapter 13.
- **The calls always include a lead.** Dawn asks questions. Kelsey says "to be fair" and gives a number.
- **Children and old people are never cute.** Owen negotiates. Frank judges. Both are usually right.
- **Chapters end on something said or done.**
- **The big moments get room.** Most paragraphs are short. Where a lead meets a feeling he or she does not understand yet, the interior runs longer and the sentences slow down (October 10 critique, and Todd's rule that cutting must not reduce interiority).
- **AI tells to keep out** (Todd): "plainly" and a narrator announcing what he is about to say; a run of "the way X does Y" similes (two or three a chapter at most); "longer than it needed" more than once; a statement followed by its own correction; "I'll give him this:" and other colon set-ups; a pair of balanced sentences that sum up a feeling; no em dashes.
- **Lines an outside reader asked to protect,** all on the page: "It is not a penalty. It is arithmetic." "I've been short my whole life. I own six ladders." "Somebody decided I did. I have never had one." "This is a table." "I had nothing in fourteen years to file that under." "It was a kind thing. I'm not telling you it wasn't. Next time, ask." "I wanted to find out what I would say." "You know how a load sounds when it's wrong." "It's strapped, boss."

## Kindle Unlimited and positioning

- **What a read is worth.** Kindle Unlimited pays by pages read. The rate was [$0.004888 a page in May 2026 and averaged $0.00445 in 2025](https://authorsgame.com/price-and-royalties/kindle-unlimited-kenp-page-read-economics); plan at $0.004, which puts an 80,000-word novel at $1.60 to $2.20 for a full read. One book rarely pays back; the series is the business case.
- **The 15% line.** Readers who pass 15% finish at much higher rates. The book opens on the first date, and it is complete by the end of chapter 2.
- **Series.** Interconnected standalones are [the most popular series structure in romance](https://fractalenigma.com/best-romance-series-kindle-unlimited-binge-2026/): the matchmaker's office, one client and one list per book. Dawn and Danny, Kelsey, and Frank (a widower on his first date in decades) are set up for their own books.
- **Back matter.** The first chapter of the next book after the epilogue, and a bonus scene for newsletter sign-ups.
- **Lead with Jo.** A 44-year-old woman with her own company and a child is the reader this book is for.
- **Cal on the cover copy.** Good-looking, well dressed and cool in public, warm on a stage; not cold, not a villain, not neurodivergent. Famous from keynotes, magazine covers and a billboard on 101, with no reporters, paparazzi or security in the book.
- **Cover:** illustrated, her truck under his billboard. The blurb and the first page show the cue card. The logline and blurb lead with the list: twelve lines, and she fails eleven. The black moment in one sentence, for pitches: he paid off the mortgage she had spent years planning to pay off herself. The blurb makes three promises: she fails eleven of his twelve lines; he falls first and keeps coming back; the gesture he thinks will prove his love is the act that nearly ends it (critique, October 9).
- **Keywords, in order:** he falls first, single mom, later in life, class difference, matchmaker; billionaire is secondary. **Avoid:** reverse age gap (three years is not what those readers want) and second chance (it means reunited exes).
- **Love scenes carry the arc:** managing in the first, present without controlling in the last.

Person, tense and heat were settled against the nearest bestsellers:

| Book | Why it is close | Person and tense | Heat |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Nora Goes Off Script*, Annabel Monaghan | Divorced mother, famous man, warm comedy | First person, present ([All About Romance](https://allaboutromance.com/book-review/nora-goes-off-script-by-annabel-monaghan-2/)) | Kisses only |
| *Part of Your World*, Abby Jimenez | Class gap, warm comedy | First person; past, as far as we know | About 2.75 of 5: three open-door scenes, one graphic ([Cleaned Up Romance](https://www.cleanedupromance.com/post/part-of-your-world)) |
| *The Fine Print*, Lauren Asher | Kindle Unlimited billionaire comedy | Dual first person ([Simply Fabs](https://simplyfabs.substack.com/p/book-review-the-fine-print-by-lauren)); present, as far as we know | 4 of 5, three or four scenes, the first past halfway ([Readify](https://readifypro.com/the-fine-print-review/)) |
| *Things We Never Got Over*, Lucy Score | Kindle Unlimited, leads aged 43 and 36 | Dual first person ([Giulia's Notes](https://giuliasnotes.substack.com/p/things-we-never-got-over-review)); past, as far as we know | Spicier than its cover, no kiss until nearly halfway ([Romancing the Phone](https://romancingthephone.substack.com/p/a-lucy-score-deep-dive)) |
| *Match Me If You Can*, Susan Elizabeth Phillips | The premise | Third person, past | 3 of 5, open door |

First person alternating, because every recent book in the table uses it. Past tense, because the bestsellers split and it suits Jo's dry asides. Heat 3, with the warm comedies nearest this book; the scenes can be made hotter later without changing the structure.

## Where outside material went

Everything below was used as situation only; nobody's lines were used as written.
- **Todd's observation bank:** "I want a wife and three kids" / "Do you want to be a husband and a father?" (Dawn's question, ch 8); stock photos kept in frames (ch 4, 20); the salt box with pebbles (the sugar bowl, ch 10); the man who tests and returns, then buys a lifetime supply (Cal's quirk); the locksmith who gets worse tips for speed (Jo's crew slows down in the last hour); private-jet regulars want a simple meal (Janice, ch 17); the girl who asks her father questions so he will talk (Abby at seven, ch 20); volume set to an even number (Janice and every dial); coffee only hot (Dawn); "any idiot can face a crisis; it's daily life that wears you out" (Frank, ch 31); the child too short by a year (ABBY 9 and OWEN 9 on the door, ch 11). Retire these from the bank as the chapters are drafted.
- **The matchmaker's channel:** the thirteenth line (nobody asks for a good apologizer; "what do you contribute"); walking her to her truck; the anonymous cruise (from a client's quiet private plane); the ten-million question; "do you really need to work this much"; the one his daughter would call; the pufferfish; washing up side by side; the phone call taken on stage; the debrief with times in it; the widower's first date, held for Frank's book; a therapist's rule that only two kinds of marriage last, equal, or the man madly more in love, because only a man that far gone does the work (Dawn to Cal, ch 32; Todd, October 12). The "no spark" sketch (a woman doubts a good man because he never makes her anxious) became Jo's problem, with Vili asking the questions in ch 7 and Kevin's silences as the comparison.
- **Clips Todd sent:** the good man who cleared a very low bar (Cal with his mother and Abby, Kevin with Owen; ch 22, 23); courtship as an investment that flips after the wedding (only as Jo's fear, through Dawn, ch 13; the book does not agree that marriage is a scam, and Kevin is not a villain); boys shamed into silence (the cafeteria, ch 34, and Owen at nine, ch 19); polite insults (the register of Dawn, Janice, Pat Dolan and Denise); cruel comebacks (not used; they became the rule that Jo is never cruel); things a man did that raised her standards (his first words in ch 10, the broken glass in ch 20 turned round so Jo does it for the dog, washing up in ch 17, who comes at 2 a.m. in ch 32, the phone face down since ch 1, his own short messages from ch 14). Left out: the stranger who pays and walks away, because in this book a gift nobody asked for is the problem.
- Candidate lines of our own for the polite-insult register, not yet placed: Dawn, "You may keep the list, Cal. I only ask that you not show it to anyone you like." Janice, "Calvin. That was a decision. It was all yours." Pat Dolan, "She talked about you all the time. Somebody had to." (Denise's "Kevin's fine. Kevin's been fine for eleven years. Ask anybody." is on the page in ch 13.)

Remy's screen says how a third party's payoff works: the lender takes payments to a loan from anyone, gives the payoff figure only to the borrower or someone she authorizes, applies the wire, refunds any excess to her and mails her the lien release, which matches 29 ([Legal Clarity](https://legalclarity.org/can-someone-pay-off-my-mortgage-tax-rules-explained/)). Added to 30: Janice, working out her notice, does only what he asks, so there is no present and no card for Abby unless he does them himself.
